{[FBIS Transcribed Text] Chairman of the Ukrainian
Parliament Aleksandr Moroz said he was in favor of the
parliamentary-presidential form of rule with a “strong
executive vertical.”

He said during his visit to the Ternopol Region of
Ukraine today that he believed that the president must
carry out only representative functions.

Moroz told employees of the Niva agricultural enterprise
that he considered some provisions of the Ukrainian
president’s decree on speeding up the land reform as
“not so well conceived and out of time.”

Ukrainian President Leonid Kuchma is now on a visit to
the U.S.

Moroz said he believed that he would propose that the
parliament veto the provisions of the decree which, in
his opinion, “lead to stealing land.”

Moroz said it would be a crime to begin selling land
“now when there is no national currency unit and there
is no money for paying for services and goods.”

FBIS-SOV-94-227
25 November 1994

At the same time, Moroz said he was in favor of
implementing the land reform but within the scope of
the documents which had already been approved. He
stressed that the Ukrainian Land Code provided for
granting the right to own land both to individual farmers
and collective farms.

Moroz also said he was in favor of continuing to subsi-
dize production of main agricultural products. He said
he believed that the federal budget must assign funds for
such subsidies as a compensation for the “nonequivalent
exchange between towns and villages.”

Belarus

Gas Supply Resumes Under ‘Strict Conditions’

WS2411094394 Minsk Radio Minsk Network in
Belarusian 0800 GMT 24 Nov 94

[FBIS Translated Text] One cannot rule out that in the
immediate future Belarus will have to pay world prices
for energy sources, declared Deputy Prime Minister
Valeryy Kokaraw at a news conference devoted to the
results of a government delegation’s recent visit to
Moscow. According to him, this assumption was
prompted by the chronic debt of Belarusian enterprises
for Russian oil and gas.

The current aggregate debt of Belarusian consumers to
Russian energy suppliers amounts to some $460 million.
In the opinion of the deputy prime minister, the first
warning from Russia’s Gazprom to the Belarusian side—
a 50 percent reduction in gas supplies to the Republic as
of 16 November—was a logical outcome.

According to Kokaraw, in the course of emergency
negotiations in Moscow, an agreement was reached on
resuming the former volume of gas supplies to Belaru-
sian pipelines in exchange for a commitment, personally
guaranteed by the government, to provide for a 100-
percent repayment of the current debt.

Another strict condition expressed by the Russian side
was the demand to present a precise schedule for the
Belarusian Government to repay its two-year energy
debt.

IMF Reconsiders Assistance

LD2311200994 Minsk Radiostantsiya Belarus
International Service in Belarusian 1900 GMT
23 Nov 94

[FBIS Translated Text] The Belarusian Government has
received an official notice from the IMF that on 15
December it is not going to consider the issue of financial
assistance to Belarus, as was planned before. The fund
has been closely following the economic policy imple-
mented in our country, the main criteria being consis-
tency of the Belarusian Government in carrying out the
anticrisis program. And while until recently there were
not any special deviations from the program of urgent
measures, approved by the Supreme Soviet and the IMF,
the well-known decree of the president on reduction of
prices, in the opinion of experts, has crossed out every-
thing positive that was developed before. Without IMF
credits, Belarus will find it extremely difficult to get out
of the abyss of the economic crisis.

In the opinion of observers, renewing relations with the
IMF will be possible in the event of resignation of the
government, which has allowed such an economically
ungrounded decision. However, the problem is that the
initiative of such an action did not come from the
government.

“The current economic crisis has coincided with the

Minister Views Foreign Economic Activities

WS$2311140094 Minsk RESPUBLIKA in Russian
17 Nov 94 p 2

[Interview with Mikhail Afanasyevich Marynich, min-
ister of foreign economic relations of Belarus, by Mikhail
Shymanskiy; place and date not given: “Belarus Should
Penetrate Into Foreign Markets”—first paragraph is
RESPUBLIKA introduction]

[FBIS Translated Text] Whatever we say, the words
“minister of foreign economic relations of Belarus”
sound proud and meaningful. We could not even dream
about such a post before, because Moscow resolved all
our issues. Thank God, times have changed.

[Shymanskiy] Well, Mikhail Afanasyevich Marynich is
the minister of foreign economic relations of Belarus. We

WESTERN REGION 47

are presenting him to our readers. It seems to me that
you see him as a laconic, severe, and even unemotional
official. He has been nominated to such a high post,
which only an expert in various areas, including diplo-
macy and behavioral norms, could occupy. Yes, we are
living at a time when poems and emotions are out of
place. But Mikhail Afanasyevich—recalling something
very dear to him—says:

[Marynich] I often visit fore.gn states due to the nature
of my work. I have to be present at various official
meetings, negotiations, receptions, and other official
ceremonies. Yes, they are living in a civilized and
luxurious world. But I am dreaming about my native
village Staryye Golovchitsy in Petrikov Rayon in Pole-
sye, where I was born. Our country’s beauty is very
serene, we have no Western luxury. We are surrounded
by birch trees, blue fir groves, and fragrant summer
flowers. Our people are kind, cordial, and industrious,
they are not spoilt by life. How can I forget about all this
when I am abroad? A poet said: ““Wherever you go, you
should never forget about your native land.”

[Shymanskiy] Mikhail Afanasyevich, you were in Prague
not long ago. You are currently working as minister of
foreign economic relations.

[Marynich] Yes, I have worked as Belarus’ ambassador
to the Czech Republic, Sie vakia, and Hungary. | had
almost settled down in Prague and plunged into my new
activities, when—all of a sudden—it was proposed that |
occupy this post. I admit that it was very difficult for me
to make this decision, and I did not accept this proposal
at first. But President Alyaksandr Lukashenka and his
team persuaded me that I—with my knowledge and
expertise in foreign trade—should head this ministry.

[Shymanskiy] Our Republic is currently trying to pene-
trate into foreign markets. What are the reasons for such
a situation?

[Marynich] First of all, our economy is very specific. It
has an open nature, more than half of our goods are
produced for export. Therefore, we should, no, we
simply must have reliable and stable partners abroad.
The second reason is the geopolitical location of Belarus.
We are, in fact, situated in the center of Europe, on the
crossing point of the most important trade routes. It
would have been illogical to fail to take advantage of this
situation.

[Shymanskiy] Foreign trade is an entirely new sector of
Belarus’ economic activity. How does our Republic cope
with new tasks?

[Marynich] Having acquired its independence and
having begun to build its own statehood, Belarus faced
many new problems. One of these problems stems from
the necessity to handle our national foreign trade more
efficiently, and to trade with the states of both the far
and near abroad. We need appropriate laws. Figuratively
speaking, we must feel at ease in foreign trade business as

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

48 WESTERN REGION

soon as possible. It is clear. Moscow alone handled
foreign trade in the former USSR. Well, there is nothing
to conceal: Belarus’ task was to produce goods, while
Moscow handled foreign trade. All the goods—produced
by Belarus—were exported through the foreign trade
network of the former USSR. Moscow had numerous
state organs—including the Foreign Trade Ministry—
and a sprawling network of trade missions and foreign
trade offices located in more than 90 countries of the
world. In addition, there were over 60 foreign trade
associations and organizations in the former USSR.
Three large research institutes, a variety of special cen-
ters and colleges drafted general concepts and strategies
for managing foreign economic activity.

It is natural that Beiarusian economic entities could not
enter foreign markets. Therefore, we lack qualified per-
sonnel. The State Committee for Foreign Economic
Relat.ons (a current ministry) was established less than
four years ago.

{Shymanskiy] What about the current situation in our
foreign trade sector?

[Marynich] We have stable econmic relations with
virtually all countries of Europe, as well as with the
United States and Japan. Not to mention the new CIS
states. In a word, our Republic is present on the markets
of all continents. Bilateral intergovernmental agree-
ments—covering trade and eccnomic cooperation—as
well as agreements on the mutual protection of invest-
ments have been signed with the majority of these states.
So, a legal and normative basis for economic cooperation
has been established.

{[Shymanskiy] Is the change of ‘he committee into a
ministry a mere change of signooards? Or was this
change of a more profound and fundamental nature?

{[Marynich] First, this change implies a higher level of
responsibility for regulating and developing foreign eco-
nomic relations. To increase the efficiency of the coor-
dinated foreign economic policy of Belarus, the ministry
provides necessary conditions for its development and
implementation. We are also coordinating and regu-
lating the foreign economic activity of ministries, depart-
ments, economic entities, and other organizations of the
Republic.

The most important issue for us is to achieve the
dynamic development of our foreign trade more quickly.
This task will be fulfilled when every economic entity of
Belarus feels at home on both foreign and domestic
markets. Has Belarus established an appropriate basis
for this process? I assume, that we have such a basis. | am
going to develop my viewpoint on this issue.

All Belarusian enterprises have received an opportunity
to independently enter foreign markets. Belarus has
chosen the correct course by liberalizing its foreign trade.
We have moved from licensing all our goods to licensing
only a few hundreds. Some 40,000 economic entities are

FBIS-SOV-94-227
25 November 1994

independently operating on foreign markets. About
1,360 joint ventures have been organized. In addition,
443 enterprises based on 100-percent foreign capital
have been registered. Their charter funds total some
$1.265 billion. Foreign investments account for almost
half of this sum. Two hundred fifty four representatives
of foreign companies have been accredited in Belarus.
Advisers on trade and economic issues, as well as our
embassies’ employees handling financial issues, are
working in 12 foreign countries.

[Shymanskiy] To make it short, Belarus is currently
trading on the markets of all continents. Do foreign
countries recognize Belarus as a reliable and stable
partner?

[Marynich] I have to admit that much should be done to
force foreign states to recognize us, to make Belarusian
goods competitive on foreign markets. This is an
extremely difficult task. World markets were divided
long ago, and it is very difficult to enter these markets
with our goods because they lose quality and other
parameters as compared with foreign goods. We should
pursue a flexible policy. Belarus should operate on the
“fields” of foreign markets, should constantly improve
its performance to make our foreign trade maximally
efficient. We should run experiments and specify our
priority tasks.

[Shymanskiy] Have you already specified our priority
tasks?

[Marynich] Of course, this is our ministry's task.

The retention of our markets in the CIS states—mainly
in Russia, Ukraine, and Kazakhstan—is our primary
concern. By the way, we are already challenged by
Germany, Austria, France, and other states on these
markets.

Every entity—from ordinary producers to enterprise
managers and politicians—should realize this problem.
The most important task is net to produce goods but to
sell them on a permanent basis to maintain our position
in our traditional markets. We should always remember
that we have not been granted these markets once and
for all; there are tough challengers. Our fight for markets
and for our presence on them is based on the quality of
our gonds, as well as on proper servicing, and, of course,
on sane prices.

Belarus—with more than half of the goods produced for
export—should not differentiate between the near and
far abroad. This task is currently of particular impor-
tance because raw materials are purchased at world
prices. Our state is beginning to implement economic
levers to regulate our foreign trade. Administrative
methods—licensing, quoting, and so on—will be cur-
tailed. This approach is supported by Belarusian Presi-
dent Alyaksandr Lukashenka, so it is a state policy.

Economic relations with East European states—former
members of the former Council for Mutual Economic

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-94-227
25 November 1994

Assistance—are our second priority task. I realized this
when I worked as an ambassador to the Czech Republic,
Slovakia, and Hungary. Our common trouble is a lack of
hard currency. Therefore, we should look for new
approaches together, and should not be afraid to begin
trading on a barter basis. This implies an exchange of
goods, the establishment of joint ventures, and other
activities. | am sure that the future of our Republic—as
well as of other CIS states—is connected with this region.

[Shymanskiy] Will the East European states trade with
us? What goods can they buy in Belarus? How will they
satisfy their needs?

{[Marynich] This is a logical question. Let us recall that
both the East European states and we were eager to have
specialized enterprises in Western markets not long ago.
This euphoria is over and we are feeling that govern-
ments and business circles are showing an ever-
increasing interest in trading with Belarus. Their interest
in the revival of trade and economic relations with
Belarus has increased. I can provide you with sufficient
examples of such cooperation. The size of our interview,
however, does not allow for citing all of them.

{Shymanskiy] Our enterprises should also be more active
and resourceful. As the saying says, no pain, no g7in....

{[Marynich] You are absolutely correct. Both our nter-
prises and organizations should come up with ore
initiatives. They should be more courageous in i” tro-
ducing goods into the markets of East European states,
especially since they have free production capacities. As
for the Czech Republic, Hungary, and Poland, they also
have a high level of technological production.

We should not forget that a number of businessmen from
East European states speak Russian; they have expertise
in cooperating with us; they are familiar with our situa-
tion and with the requirements of our market. They are
showing interest in cooperating with Belarusian enter-
prises. The proximity of our states—resulting in minimal
expenditures on transportation—is also an argument for
widening export and import of goods, labor, and services
among our states. To make it short, we should operate
actively. This will prompt an active response.

The states of the Mediterranean Region, the Near East,
North Africa, and Southeast Asia are our third priority
task. Regrettably, we are strangers to these states. Anal-
ysis of these regions shows that we could sell our goods
there and make considerable profits.

[Shymanskiy] Mikhail Afanasyevich, how do we find
access to overseas markets. I am primarily referring to
the United States.

[Marynich] The Us.ited States is an international finan-
cial center, therefore it is very important for little
Belarus to develop our trade and economic relations
with this state. Both our states are enjoying the most-
favored-nation trading status and we have worked hard
to achieve such results. This situation, however, could

WESTERN REGION 49

accelerate the adoption of similar decisions by other
members of the international community. According to
statistics, the U.S. markets are open to Belarusian goods,
and our bilateral trade is developing dynamically. The
United States holds third place in trading with Belarus
after Germany and Poland, on the markets of which we
are selling the largest amount of our goods. We have
exported goods for a total of some $20 million to
Germany and Poland in the first half of 1994 (this
amount accounts for half of our exports in 1993).

I would like to apeak about our economic relations with
Germany which holds first place among our trade part-
ners. Our trade turnover with Germany is growing with
each year: It stood at $103 million in 1991, while in 1993
it was $227 million. The volume of Belarusian export
deliveries to Germany is also increasing: The rates of
export growth stood at 18.3 and 56.6 percent in 1991 and
1993, respectively. If we interpret these dry figures along
practical lines, every sixth dollar in the overall value of
Belarusian exports is in our trade relations with Ger-
many.

{[Shymanskiy] What are the primary tasks of the foreign
economic relations sector, let us say, in the immediate
two to three years?

[Marynich] Let us discuss three large groups of such
tasks. They are as follows: Foreign trade, direct invest-
ments and attracting credits, and development of the
normative-legal basis.

Regarding the first group, it is necessary to stimulate the
development of both exports and imports. We are going
to decrease the share of barter deals, develop and imple-
ment the system of registering foreign trade operations.
Such measures will toughen control over the prompt
return of hard currency profits to Belarus. We have also
outlined a variety of other important measures.

Foreign investments are of specific importance for the
development of our economy. The system of control
over the use and settlement of foreign loans—in partic-
ular, of those the repayment of which has been guaran-
teed by the government—should be developed. Proce-
dures for specifying the participation of foreign investors
in the privatization of state property should be simpli-
fied. The reliability of guarantees for foreign investors
should, of course, be increased.

I will not discuss in detail the third group of our tasks.
We should work hard to develop the normative-legal
basis for our foreign economic activities.

[Shymanskiy] What moral qualities, in your opinion,
should the officers of your ministry have?

[Marynich] Honesty and decency. High professional
qualities are also required. Foreign states primarily form
an opinion about Belarus based on the activities our
ministry officers. Therefore, we should observe honor
and dignity of our state.

Ministry Issues Directive on Short-Term Bonds

WS$2511091894 Minsk Radio Minsk Network in
Belarusian 0800 GMT 25 Nov 94

{[FBIS Translated Text] According to a directive of the
Belarusian Cabinet of Ministers, the Finance Ministry
has been instructed to issue the fourth parcel of short-
term state bonds worth 20 billion rubles [R] with an
85-day circulation term. The nominal value of each bond
is R10,000. The directive states that only the Belarusian
Stock Exchange and the Interbank Foreign Currency
Stock Exchange are authorized to handle short-term
state bonds on the secondary [druhasny] market.

Army on Structure Details, Kastenka Profiled

WS2311122994 Minsk BELAPAN in English 1800
GMT 21 Nov 94

(Unattributed report from the “Analysis, Commentaries,
Forecasts” feature No. 6 15-21 November]

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

52 WESTERN REGION

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] In accordance with the law “On
the Armed Forces of the Republic of Belarus,” the
Belarusian Armed Forces consist of the Land Forces, Air
Forces, Antiaircraft Forces, special troops, military edu-
cational institutions, as well as the departments, organi-
zations and enterprises of the Ministry of Defense.

The Land Forces consist of three army corps.

The 28th Army Corps (AC) is stationed on the territories
of Grodno Oblast and Brest Oblast (its headquarters is
situated in Grodno). The 28th AC comprises 6th, 11th
and 50th detached mechanized army brigades, an arma-
ment and equipment base as well as a number of corps
units, including missile units, antiaircraft units, chem-
ical and engineer troops, signal troops and elements of
the corps’ rear services.

The 65th AC is stationed on the territories of Minsk
Oblast and Vitebsk Oblast (its headquarters is situated in
Borisov). It comprises three armament and equipment
bases and a number of corps units.

The Sth Guards Army Corps has two armament and
equipment bases, a number of corps units and the 30th
detached mechanized brigade. The Sth AC is stationed
on the territory of Minsk Oblast and Mogilev Oblast (its
headquarters is situated in Bobruysk).

If necessary, the armament and equipment bases may be
transformed into combat formations and units. In accor-
dance with Belarus’ military doctrine, such structures are
able to perform the tasks of flexible and active defense.

A brigade comprises 4-5 battalions, is 3,000-5,000
people strong (the overall strength of a former division
was up to 14,000 people), and is not inferior to a former
division in fire power. But the brigade is a formation for
defense whereas divisions were formed for offensive
operations.

The Air Forces comprise aircraft units for various pur-
poses, they are able to perform tasks on protecting the
most important establishments, provide air support to
land forces, conduct air reconnaissance, radioelectronic
combat, air transportation of troops and equipment, and
others. They consist of air bases for various purposes and
detached units of protection.

The Antiaircraft Forces perform tasks on protecting
administrative, political, industrial centers, groups of the
Armed Forces, important military and other establish-
ments from enemy’s air attacks. In peace and war time
the Antiaircraft Forces perform their missions in a single
system formed in cooperation with the antiaircraft for-
mations and units of the Land Forces. They comprise
antiaircraft missile brigades, antiaircraft missile regi-
ments, detached groups of antiaircraft defense, units of
radioelectronic reconnaissance.

The Special Troops are aimed at providing the combat
activities of the Armed Forces and performing their own
distinctive special tasks. They include reconnaissance

FBIS-SOV-94-227
25 November 1994

formations and units, engineer and chemical troops,
signal troops, units for radioelectronic combat, forma-
tions and units of technical support, road-building, and
motor-car units. They are an element of the Land Forces
and the Rear of the Armed Forces.

The Rear of the Armed Forces performs the tasks of rear
support to the Armed Forces.

Control over the Armed Forces is exercised by the
Ministry of Defense that bears all responsibility for their
development and preparation for performing tasks.
Anatol Kastenka is Minister of Defense of Belarus.

Minister of Defense Anatol Kastenka—Some Traits

Colonel-General Kastenka has made a brilliant career
working his way up from platoon commander to com-
mander in chief of the Belarusian Military District. In
the district he served as battalion commander, chief of
staff in a regiment and division commander.

His appointment Minister of Defense met with a mixed
reaction among regular officers. Some say he is a source
of hopes for introducing good order and proper disci-
pline in the Army, for toughening struggle against cor-
ruption among high-ranked commanding officers.
Others maintain that the president should have selected
a fresh man for ruling the Belarusian Armed Forces, the
more so since, in accordance with order No. 0903 dated
December 28, 1992, Kastenka was transferred to the
reserve. In the world military practice there is no prece-
dent for Kastenka’s appointment Minister of Defense
directly from the reserve.

It is supposed that the 54-year-old minister, most likely,
will conduct the policy of introducing proper discipline
and improving professional skill. Kastenka believes that
the Belarusian Army, as an element of the former Soviet
Union’s Armed Forces has good traditions and an abun-
dant experience, which should be used. He also pointed
out repeatedly that he would make every effort to restore
the dignity and honor of the officer’s profession in the
finest sense of the word.

Kastenka has two sons. Both of them are officers. The
Minister's hobby is tennis. In his spare time, he prefers
to go in for sports. The minister has not rejected the
statement by the SVABODA newspaper that he has a
“white card” (unfit for military service). He confirms
that a medical commission pronounced him unfit for
military service in peace-time, but he mentions that “this
is a complicated problem.” Kastenka also confirms that
he, having assumed the office, never pledged allegiance
to Belarus: “The president has appointed me minister,
he has not called up me for active service. That is why
not being on the active list I have no right to take the
oath”.

His attitude toward the Partnership for Peace program is
rather positive. But as for Belarus’ joining it, he main-
tains that Belarus should not be in a hurry. In an
interview to ZV YAZDA he declared: ‘““We may subscribe

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination |
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-94-227
25 November 1994

to the program when we determine the degree of our
participation in it. This degree may be different: from a
full- scaie participation, including the organization of
joint large-scale exercises and so on, to a participation
amounting to nothing more than an exchange of iffor-
mation or military school students. For us the economic
aspect is the most important. This arrangement is expen-
sive enough and we should calculate precisely ,all
expenses and all incomes, weigh the pros and cons.”

New Union Looks To Unify Moldovan Ukrainians

AU2411184494 Chisinau BASAPRESS in English 1930
GMT 23 Nov 94

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Chisinau BASAPRESS, 23/
11/1994—Viktor Grebenshikov, presidential counselor
in national minorities’ issues, relayed to BASA that last
week, in Iablona, Glodeni district, the Moldovan Ukrai-
nians’ Union was created. It is headed by Moldovan
Deputy Nicolae Oleinic. Oleinic told BASA that the
Union was created to unify all Ukrainian cultural soci-
eties and consolidate the friendship between Moldovans
and native Ukrainians. The Union will support the
development of the Moldovan Ukrainians’ culture and
traditions which have much in common with the Mold-
ovan traditions. National Minorities Department
reports that five Ukrainian national-cultural organiza-
tions were registered in Moldova: “Prosvita” Teachers
Society, Ukrainian Painters Union, Ukrainian Cultural
Society, Ukrainian Women’s Society, and “Moldova-
Ukraine” Friendship Society. All these organizations
have a coordinating council, whose chairperson is Svet-
lana Myslitskaya, Chisinau deputy mayor.

Ministry Denies Involvement at Vulcanesti

AU2311161694 Chisinau INFOTAG in English 2000
GMT 21 Nov 94

{FBIS Transcribed Text) Chisinau INFOTAG, 21/
11/1994—The Moldovan deputy interior minister,
Mihai Gorincioi, resolutely refuted the accusations of
the Gagauz republic that the last week’s armed incident
in Vulcanesti had been provoked by Chisinau.

As was already informed by INFOTAG, the incident
resulted in two Moldovan policemen wounded and three
Gagauz guardsman seriously injured, with one of the
latter having died shortly after.

The leadership of the self-styled Gagauz republic has
regarded the case as a purposeful political provocation of

WESTERN REGION 53

Chisinau aimed to postpone the adoption of the Gagauz
status law at the second reading expected last week.

The Moldovan deputy interior minister said to
INFOTAG that the bloody incident in South Moldova
was a purely criminal case. On November 11, two
Vulcanesti residents, Kirizi and Kostev, had beaten up
the police commissar of the Vulcanesti area, Captain
Franciu. The area’s prosecutor initialed proceedings.
However, the police then failed to arrest the two armed
criminals wanted for several more crimes.

In this connection, an Interior Ministry’s emergency
group was sent to the area to seize them. The group
detected them in a car. They did not only disobeyed the
police’s order to stop, but opened back fire. During the
shooting, two policemen were heavily wounded, as well
as Kirizi, Kostev and their driver.

Kirizi died shortly after. Kostev is in a hospital in Comrat,
and the driver is in a military hospital in Bolgrad
(Ukraine). The two wounded Moldovan policemen are
now in a Comrat hospital, and the Gagauz administration
is refusing to hand them over to Chisinau.

Mihai Gorincioi stressed there was no politics in the
incident, and that viewpoint was shared by Gagauz
leaders, including Gagauz president Stepan Topal,
whom Gorincioi met with.

“However, certain political forces in Comrat are taking
advantage of the fact that one of the criminals was a
member of the Gagauz guards, and seeking to make a
political affair of the incident. Appropriate investigation
will clarify the truth. However, even now one can say the
above efforts are doomed to failure,” Deputy Interior
Minister said.

Kazakhstan

Uranium Deal With United States Confirmed

LD2311133594 Moscow ITAR-TASS in English 1311
GMT 23 Nov 94

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Alma-Ata [Almaty] November
23 TASS—The Kazakh Foreign Ministry confirmed on
Wednesday [23 November] that Alma-Ata and Wash-
ington had reached an agreement to transfer some 600
kilogrammes of highly enriched uranium stored at the
Ulbinskiy metallurgical plant in Kazakhstan to the
United States.

“Upon the initiative of Kazakhstan which has concluded
an agreement with the International Atomic Energy
Agency (IAEA) regarding full-scale guarantees, the ura-
nium is transferred to the USA which is a country-
depository of the nuclear Non-Proliferation Treaty for
purposes of diminishing the risk of proliferation of such
weapons”, the ministry said in a statement.

“This step is undertaken in the context of measures
drafted by Kazakhstan to ensure the safety of its nuclear
materials and reduce to the minimum the risk of their
proliferation”, the ministry said.

Russia Said Aware of Uranium Sale

LD2411120394 Moscow INTERFAX in English 1142
GMT 24 Nov 94

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] Kazakhstan’s agreement to
move 600 kg of highly-enriched uranium to the United
States was not an unpleasant surprize for Russia, the
press service chief of the Russian Atomic Energy Min-
istry has said. Georgiy Kaurov explained that Russia was
notified of Kazakhstan’s intention to sell the uranium
and even invited to buy it back in 1993.

Kaurov explained that when the USSR broke up, the
Ulbinskii metallurgical plant making fuel assemblies for
nuclear reactors found itself in an independent Kaza-
khstan. At that time the plant had 167 kg of a beryllium
alloy with 90-percent enriched uranium-235 and about
28 kg of uranium dioxide with a 90-percent enrichment
for uranium-235.

Kaurov said that the fuel assemblies were made for
nuclear reactors of submarines and transport ships of the
first generation. Fuel of that type is no longer in use.

In 1993 Kazakhstan invited Russia to purchase the
uranium for $14,000 a kilo. However, Russia rejected
the offer, because it has enough nuclear materials of its
own and lacks the know-how to extract pure uranium
from the beryllium alloy to make nuclear arms.

Kaurov said that during the Russian government dele-
gation’s visit to the United States last December the
American side recommended the Russian Atomic

' CENTRAL ASIA 55

Energy Minister Viktor Mikhaylov to purchase the ura-
nium from Kazakhstan to lessen the risk of its prolifer-
ation. Another option was offered, too. The United
States would purchase the uranium from Kazakhstan
and hand it over to Russia for safe- keeping. Mikhaylov
rejected that proposal.

Kaurov said that the uranium could not be used to make
nuclear arms. He said that extracting pure uranium from
the alloy would be extremely difficult. He said he did not
rule out the Americans were ignorant [that they] were
buying a uranium-beryllium alloy.

Russian-Kazakh Oil Talks Held in Almaty

LD2311113794 Almaty PANORAMA in Russian
19 Nov 94 p7

[FBIS Translated Text} This week a delegation from the
Russian state company Rosneft [Russian Oil] visited
Almaty. During the visit the guests, together with repre-
sentatives of the Muniegaz [Oil and Gas] company,
discussed a number of joint projects, in particular, on an
oil exchange between Russia and Kazakhstan. To keep
the oil refineries of both countries fully occupied Russia
will supply hydrocarbon raw materials to the plants of
Chimkent and Pavlodar and Kazakhstan will supply the
Samara plant as was done during the USSR period.
Annually each side will supply around four million
tonnes of oil.

However possible cooperation between the Kazakh and
Russian oil companies is not limited just to this. The
Russians have expressed an interest in buying Kazakh
coal, corn and non-ferrous metals. Rosneft can pay for
these goods in oil and petroleum products. This pro-
posal, evidently, will arouse the interest of the Kazakh
side. Besides Rosneft is looking for a new market in
Kazakhstan which is less saturated than the Russian
market. The company intends to sell about four million
tonnes of various petroleum products in our republic
annually. The Russian oil industry workers who have
free technical and personnel resources also offered the
Kazakh side their assistance in the capital repair of oil
wells and in the exploitation of 1-2 oil deposits. The
specific deposits have not been determined yet but
Kazakhstan’s interest in the proposals is evident.

The local specialists for their part are ready to propose
that the Russians participate in joint oil prospecting on
the Caspian Sea shelf. Taking into consideration the
situation around the oil riches of the Caspian Sea of late,
one can surmise that Rosneft will accept the proposal.
As yet the mutual proposals are intentions, but if they are
realized, then Kazakh-Russian relations in the oil sphere
will lose their confrontational nature and will really
become the relations of partners.

the end of this year.

out the construction of a pipeline to transport the oil
being extracted in Kazakhstan’s sector of the Caspian

nating functions upon itself to carry out the project.

EBRD ’s intentions. At the same time he expressed the
desire that all the formalities connected with giving the
planned credits should be speedily concluded.

Westling assured him that the bank would make the
appropriate changes to the plans concerning Kazakhstan.
However, in his turn he proposed that work be intensi-
fied on improving the legislative basis of the republic as
regards providing more reliable protection for foreign
investments and on creating a suitable material stimulus.

Jacek Bzhezinskiy, the head of the bank’s Kazakhstan
mission, which opened a few days ago in Almaty, took
part in the conversation.

Court Dismisses Case Against Russian Journalist

LD2411121294 Almaty KAZAKHSTANSKAYA
PRAVDA in Russian 19 Nov 94 p3

{[FBIS Translated Text] The Supreme Court of the
Republic of Kazakhstan has considered the appeal by
journalist Boris Suprunyuk and his lawyers against the
decision of the Northern Kazakhstan regional court of 2
September 1994. A citizen of the Russian Federation,
Boris Suprunyuk was found guilty of committing a crime
covered by Article 60 of the Criminal Code of the

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

FBIS-SOV-94-227
25 November 1994

Republic of Kazakhstan—the premeditated public dis-
semination of opinions and ideas undermining confi-
dence in and respect for the Kazakh people and stirring
up ethnic discord. He was given a sentence of two years
imprisonment suspended for two years. During the
investigation Boris Suprunyuk spent about six weeks in
detention.

The journalist and his lawyer appealed against the deci-
sion calling it illegal and pointing out the inadmissibility
of the practice of prosecuting citizens for their convic-
tions. The collegium of the Supreme Court examined
several folders containing articles from dozens of
Kazakh and Russian newspapers, including speeches by
officials, as evidence of wrong decisions being taken on
social and ethnic issues in Kazakhstan which cause
migration of the Russian-speaking population and other
negative actions. Just listing such facts took more than
an hour and probably the most harmless and curious of
them was the renaming of “Russian” vodka in Petropav-
losk—now called “Kyzylzhar” vodka.

“I spoke and wrote only about what is around us,” Boris
Suprunyuk said, “but I was arrested and convicted on
political grounds in order to be shown as an example.
Instead of prosecuting the true culprits of today’s disas-
trous situation for Kazakh, Russian and other peoples
residing in Kazakhstan, the authorities decided to
punish as an example the journalist who called a spade a
spade.”

Having studied the materials of the case, the collegium,
led by the first deputy chairman of the Supreme Court of
the Republic of Kyrgyzstan, M. Narikbayev, allowed the
appeal and overruled the decision of the Northern Kaza-
khstan regional court and halted the case as there was no
corpus delicti in Boris Suprunyuk’s actions.

Thus justice has triumphed. Now Boris Suprunyuk has
the right to raise the issue of the accountability of
officials who have violated the law and also demand
damages for the moral harm caused to him by the bodies
of law and order. The question remains: will the right
conclusions be drawn from this by all interested parties?

Russian Units Include Military Intelligence

LD2311141494 Bishkek VECHERNIY BISHKEK in

[FBIS Translated Text] According to information from a
confidential source, the so-called (in specialist jargon)

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination

58 CENTRAL ASIA

third line of the group of Russian border guards in
Kyrgyzstan has been considerably strengthened. We are
talking about the military counter-intelligence direc-
torate. Its personnel has been increased several times
over—up to 49 people.

For comparison the similar service of the Ministry of
Defense of Kyrgyzstan—the information and analysis cen-

charged with obtaining intelligence data about the inten-

trying to increase their influence in Central Asia.

Time will show how much this action works for the
consolidation of the Kyrgyz-Chinese frontier. By the way
measures like this in no way contradict the inter-state
accord on the status of the group of Russian border
guards in our republic.

Osh Region Has 1,900 Refugees From Tajikistan
LD2311115994

[FBIS Editorial Report) Osh EKHO OSHA in Russian
on 12 November carries on page | an unattributed report
on refugees in Kyrgyzstan from Tajikistan. The report
begins by noting that Kyrgyzstan’s Osh region has a
population of 1,460,000, of whom 720,000 are men and
740,000 women; 669,000 people able to work, 538,200
of whom are engaged in production and 81,500 of whom
are not working.

The report goes on to state that by 25 September 1994
there were 392 refugee families, or 1,866 refugees, from the
Tajik Republic in Osh region. Their ethnic composition,
the paper states, is as follows: 1,483 ethnic Kyrgyz, 200
Uzbeks, 175 Tajiks, four Russians, and four Tartars.

For the time being, the paper writes, 420,000 soms out of
the amount which the UN High Commissioner for
Refugees promised after visiting the refugees in June
1993 and August 1994 has been distributed in the
districts where the refugees have settled.

The report conciudes by noting that local authorities are
taking all the necessary measures to provide the refugees
with food, medical services, and jobs.

FBIS-SOV-94-227
25 November 1994

Participants include private farmers, the heads of min-

for Reconstruction and Development, the United
Nations Development Program, the international fund
for agricultural development, and so on.

In his welcoming address to participants in the conference,
Kyrgyz President Askar Akayev noted that the reforms
currently being carried out in the republic’s agricultural
industry are of a make-or-break nature, because the agri-
cultural sector is a priority for Kyrgyzstan. Success of the
reforms would mean a substantial reduction in unemploy-
ment and an increase in the living standards of the
majority of the population. The task is now to implement
the third part of the reform as fast as possible, bearing in
mind all the errors made in earlier phases of the reform.

This conference must serve as a kind of catalyst for
developing further reform policy for agriculture, and
must clarify the strategic areas for this policy. We are
cooperating closely with many international organiza-
tions, which are of invaluable assistance to our work.
Above all we should mention the World Bank and its
representative, (Michael Guld), the head of the bank’s
agriculture, industry, and finance department.

The laws and decrees passed in the republic and the
resolutions issued by the government create solid legal
foundations for the reforms, open up broad scope for
them, and confirm that the policy the republic has
chosen is irreversible.

Abbas Dzhumagulov presented a well-grounded report. He
described the stages which the republic has gone through in
the reform process, assessed them, pointed out all the
positive things which have already been achieved, and
raised a number of problems yet to be solved. The prime
minister spoke about the prospects of the development of
sheep and other livestock farming and tobacco and cotton
growing under market and private enterprise conditions.
He also spoke about the need to develop processing
industries and about steps to radically reorganize manage-
ment structures, specifically within the Ministry of Agri-
culture and Food. He dwelt in detail on the assistance
which international organizations and foreign countries
are giving Kyrgyzstan to implement the reforms.

bekov; farmers Imanaliyev, Kim, and Akmatov; the head
of Dzhumgarskiy district state administration, Samudi-
nov; the deputy head of Osh Region state administration
Osmonov; Fine ice Minister Nanayev; and others.

The conference continues today.

the Academies of Sciences of China and Tajikistan.

[From the “Presidential Bulletin” feature]

{[FBIS Transcribed Text] President Emomali Rakh-
manov has welcomed yesterday's move by former mili-

weapons at Tursun-zade 60 kilometers west of the capital
Dushanbe. He described it as the start of general disar-

trial center and has the world’s third largest aluminum
plant.

In an interview with Interfax, the Tajik leader expressed
the hope that the process of surrender of illegal weapons
will be speeded up. Rakhmanov says that an overall
disarmament and the disbanding of illegal armed units is
his main task.

Commander of the CIS peacekeeping force in Tajikistan
Valeriy Patrikyev attending the ceremony of weapons
surrender spoke of common sense now prevailing in the
republic.

He expressed the hope that other rebel units would
follow suit. Patrikyev pledged that his force would work
together with the Tajik Government and people to try to
avert any fratricide there in future.

iF [Interfax] Note: Two years ago, part of the residents of
Tursun-zade joined the rauks of rebel units of the
People’s Front set up im August 1992 “to defend the
constitution against attacks by Islamic fundamentalists
and allied democrats”. By early 1993, the well-armed
rebel force numbered some 2,500 members in Gissar
Valley alone. When the country’s civil war ended in the
same year, the rebels surrendered about 90 percent of
their weapons to police units and security forces. But
they held back to some 1,000 units of firearms and
ammunition, according to the security ministry.

Deputy Interior Minister Almirkul Azimov described
the latest arms surrender as the first serious step on the
way to national reconciliation and glaring evidence of
support for the president who called for all problems to
be resolved in a peaceful way. In an interview with
Interfax, Azimov said that after inspection and repair,
the surrendered weapons and armor would be stored or
used on the Afghan border.

country’s Supreme Council of the 12th convocation. The
heads of ministries, departments, large industrial enter-
prises, national and commercial banks, and commercial
structures of the country were present at the meeting.

Valuable proposals about bringing legal documents into
line with the demands of the market economy were put
forward at the meeting. The importance of further
improving customs and tax policy, as well as creating
favorable conditions for foreign investments in the
national economy were emphasized. It was mentioned at
the meeting that without the liberalization of foreign
economic trade it is impossible to achieve fruitful results
in trading.

Specific proposals submitted during the meeting are
being thoroughly studied in the president’s administra-
tion. These and other proposals arriving from various
regions of the republic will be collected and they will be
used as a basis for the practical work on accelerating
economic transformations in Tajikistan.

UN Mission Opers in Ashgabat

LD2511101394 Ashgabat TURKMENISTAN in
Turkmen 11 Nov 94 pl

[“UN Mission Opened in Ashgabat”}

{[FBIS Translated Excerpt] On 8 November Omer
Ertyur, the UN representative in Turkmenistan, pre-
sented his credentials to our foreign minister, Khalyk-
berdy Atayey, and commenced his duties.

The opening of the UN mission in Ashgabat will make it
possible to coordinate all the UN structures and agencies
operating in Turkmenistan and resolve many problems
arising during the transition period on the spot. Mr. Ertyur
said that most attention should be paid to matters in the
social sphere. In line with this UN experts together with
scholars from the Academy of Sciences of Turkmenistan
have already been carrying out work within the framework
of the world-wide program - indices of development of the
human factor. Research is already being carried out in
such vitally important fields as ecology, health and educa-
tion and the results will be given to the UN.

The organization of a system for transferring theoretical
knowledge will be a major aspect of the work of UN
structures in Turkmenistan. As Mr. Ertyur said graphi-
cally: ““We do not give people fish, we teach them how to
fish.” [passage omitted]

Currently the UN mission is working on a program of
technical and financial assistance to Turkmenistan. The
social and economic fields in the national-economic
complex will be priorities in this program and much
attention will be paid to ecological problems. Moreover,
as Mr. Ertyur noted, the Turkmen government has the
final say in determining the fields of cooperation.

CENTRAL ASIA 61

involving private and mixed funds in Tashkent and

Turkmenistan’s new banking system has in the main
been formed. As in all other CIS countries, it was set up
on the relevant legal foundations and carries out its
activities at two levels: With the Central Bank at the high
level and with joint-stock and commercial banks at a
lower level.

The establishment of the Turkmenistan International
Bank for Reconstruction, Development, and Enterprise
can be described as a product of the open doors policy
announced by the Turkmen President Saparmyrat Turk-
menbashy [Saparmurad Niyazov]. Its tasks are to pro-
mote exports of Turkmen oil and gas to world markets
and, related to this, to help construct transnational
pipelines and find and supervise the investments needed
to reconstruct our refining industry. In addition, of
course, it envisages helping to increase production of
cotton, grain, fruit and vegetables and to back the
republic’s agricultural industry complex.

The establishment of free enterprise economic zones has
borne fruit. Currently 711 industrial objects are being
built in Turkmenistan. One in seven is an enterprise
involving foreign firms, and 30 involve foreign capital.
We are thus using credit to render comprehensive assis-
tance to these construction projects. At the same time, in
addition to funding these projects and rendering finan-
cial services we are helping to reconstruct old industrial
capacities.

The board of our bank is trying to live up to expecta-
tions, and to be worthy of its name and similar to the
European bank. We are working in close cooperation
with the World Bank in Washington, Asian Develop-
ment Bank (Manila), the African Development Bank, the
Is Bank and Halk Bank in Turkey, and other similar
specialized international banking organizations.

Nor are we standing aside from banking integration in
the CIS countries. The Cabinet of Ministers has
instructed us to establish direct cooperation with Rus-
sian joint-stock and commercial banks. A branch of the
Rossiyskiy Credit bank will open in Ashkhabad [Ash-
gabat] in the near future, and we are currently con-
ducting negotiations with the following Moscow banks:
Kredobank, Menatep, Neftekhimbank, and Neftegazos-
troybank.

stages. Implementation of the tasks involved in each
stage are directed toward one main goal—rapid comple-
tion of the buildings belonging to institutions in which
we invest. For this purpose the Turkmenistan Interna-
tional Bank for Reconstruction, Development and

Natural Gas Supply Stopped for Nonpayment

LD2411174594 Moscow INTERFAX in English 1617
GMT 24 Nov 94

[FBIS Transcribed Text] 1 Thursday [24 November]
Uzbekistan stopped shipments of natural gas to Kyr-
gyzstan explaining this by non-payments on the part of
Kyrgyzstan, a well-informed source in the Uzbek Gov-
ernment reported to Interfax.

In accordance with the agreement on trade-economic
cooperation between two neighboring states signed on
January 16, 1994 in Bishkek this year Uzbekistan was to
deliver 1.4 billion cubic meters of natural gas to Kyr-
gyzstan.

According to the industrial association Uztransgaz, as of
October 1, 1994 the republic has shipped 687.929 mil-
lion cubic meters of gas. Kyrgyzstan’s debt for natural
gas only on this trade-economic agreement concluded in
1994 totalled $5 million.

Official sources from Kyrgyzstan report that as of today
Kyrgyzstan’s debt to Uzbekistan for the gas delivered in
1993-1994 is $7.5 million.

Kyrgyzstan’s Industry and Trade Minister Andrey
lordan said that now the republic is urgently looking for
money to pay off the debt. He believes that Kyrgyzstan’s
debt to Uzbekistan could have been liquidated if Kaza-
klistan had paid to Kyrgyzstan for electric power being
consumed by this republic.

Start of Limited Land Privatization Decreed

LD2411174294 Tashkent Uzbekistan Television in
Uzbek 1400 GMT 24 Nov 94

{FBIS Translated Text] By edict of the president of the
Republic of Uzbekistan, and with the aim of increasing
the effectiveness of land use in the republic and
increasing the interest of the population in the growth in
incomes from plots of land, town and district adminis-
trations are allowed to sell at auction plots of land of up
to 0.4 hectares [ha] for individual house building and up
to 0.6 ha for subsistence farming to citizens for life-long
hereditary ownership.

As an experiment, in the Republic of Karakalpakstan
plots of land that have no economic use, and also land
belonging to unprofitableo and barely profitable agricul-
tural enterprises, may be sold at auction and on a
competition basis to citizens for life-long hereditary
ownership.

The full text of the edict by the republic’s president,
Islam Karimov, on increasing the effectiveness of land
use will be published in the press.

Edict Issued on Land Sales

LD2511100894 Tashkent NARODNOYE SLOVO in
Russian 25 Nov 94 pl

[“Edict of the President of the Republic of Uzbekistan
on Improving the Efficiency of Land Use’’]

[FBIS Translated Text] With the aim of improving the
efficiency of land use in the republic and increasing the
interest of the population in the growth in incomes from
plots of land:

1. District and town administrations are allowed to sell
at auction plots of land up to 0.04 hectares in area for
private house building and up to 0.06 hectares in area for
private subsistence farming to citizens for permanent
hereditary ownership.

2. As an experiment, in the Republic of Karakalpakstan
plots of land which have no economic use and also land
belonging to unprofitable and barely profitable agricul-
tural enterprises may be sold at auction and on a
competition basis to citizens for permanent hereditary

Azerbaijan Reportedly Fires on Armenian Towns

NC2311191594 Yerevan Radio Yerevan in Azeri 1430
GMT 23 Nov 94

{[FBIS Translated Text] The situation on Nagorno-
Karabakh’s frontline is relatively peaceful. Azerbaijan
has frequently disturbed the peace on its border with
Armenia. Azerbaijan’s military units have fired on sev-
eral border areas with various types of arms for no
reason at all, hoping that they will provoke the Armenian
side to act in the same way. But they have failed to
achieve their objective. The Armenian side has remained
cautious. It has not been carried away by the provoca-
tion.

The Armenian Defense Ministry Information Service
has revealed that Azerbaijan’s military units fired on
defense positions in Vahan and Champarak in Kras-
noselskiy Rayon with various types of arms on the night
of 21 November. No one was hurt on the Armenian side.
The Armenian border guards did not return the fire.

Azerbaijan’s officials purport to support the peace talks.
But they condone the activities of their military units,
activities that may worsen the situation. They create the
conditions their military units need to behave in that
way. Had that not been the case, Azerbaijan’s military
units would have taken care to heed the cease-fire
agreement.

Officials in Nagorno-Karabakh and Armenia have done
everything they could to find a peaceful solution to the
dispute. The Armenian defense forces have been care-
fully complying with the cease-fire agreement in
Nagorno-Karabakh and on the border between Armenia
and Azerbaijan.

Azerbaijan
IMF Postpones Signing Credit Agreement
LD2411210794 Moscow INTERFAX in English 1923
GMT 24 Nov 94

[FBIS Transcribed Text] The International Monetary
Fund (IMF) and Azerbaijan have postponed signing a
credit agreement worth $160 million from November to
January 1995. State Economic Policy Advisor Vakhid

Akhundov said Thursday [24 November] that the step ~

was caused by the republic’s failure so far to comply with
the main IMF requirements.

In an interview with the Financial Information Agency,
he said that the IMF insisted on reducing the budget
deficit in 1994 and stabilizing the national monetary
unit. However, the officially set rate of the Azerbaijani
manat has fallen from 950 to 3,000 per one U.S. dollar

since August. The budget deficit, planned to be kept ©

down to 7.5% of GDP, has risen to 20% of GDP by late
November.

According to Akhundov, the manat’s drop was caused by
the National Bank’s mass crediting of agriculture, oil
production and machine-building. Sources in the gov-
ernment estimated the credits at 60 billion manats
($63.1 million at the bank’s August rate).

Akhundov pointed out that IMF experts had come to the
republic to look into the economic situation and jointly
prepare a memorandum on Azerbaijan’s cooperation
with the IMF. He described the requirements contained
in the memorandum as “rather strict.” The budget
deficit is to be brought down to 5-7% of GDP in the
beginning of reforms and 1-2% of GDP when the sys-
temic transformation will be over.

IMF experts also recommend to make the National
Bank’s refinancing rate dependent on the inflation rate.
(At present, the bank’s annual refinancing rate is 150%,
while the inflation rate has reached 910% over the first
ten months of 1994).

If IMF conditions are met, Azerbaijan will be granted a
systemic transformation facility worth $160 million. In
addition, the IMF credit may be followed by another one
worth over $400 millior from the World Bank.

Ap to World Community Over Plight of
Refugees

LD2411222994 Moscow ITAR-TASS World Service in
Russian 1719 GMT 24 Nov 94

[By ITAR-TASS correspondent Sevindsh Abdullayeva,
Viktor Shulman]

FBIS-SOV-94-227
25 November 1994

[FBIS Translated Text] Baku, 24 Nov—Despite its
promises, the world community is not paying due atten-
tion to the plight of more than | million Azerbaijani
refugees, many of whom have been living in tents and
shelters in the open steppe in the heart of the republic for
a second year running, after a considerable part of
Azerbaijan’s territory was occupied. President of Azer-
baijan Geydar Aliyev said this today at the closing
session of an international conference on refugee legis-
lation, human rights, and migration.

Speaking to the participants in the forum from 10
countries and 34 international organizations, the Azer-
baijani leader said that he intended to raise this question
once again with the UN High Commission for Refugees.
The refugee issue needs to be resolved as quickly as
possible, Aliyev said, since no other conflict zone in the
wurld has produced such depressing statistics on the
people who have lost their homes. Every seventh resi-
dent of Azerbaijan has now become a refugee, he said.
According to the president, the fact that they are refugees
in their own country adds to the tragedy.

Aliyev said that he was going to appeal to other major
international organizations and political leaders of influ-
ential countries to help the republic resolve the refugee
issue: The civilized world can and must assist Azerbaijan
in getting over this tragedy and to protect the rights of
the people living there, he said.

for Norway.

Murvang heads the Norwegian delegation which arrived
in Baku on November 21. The delegation includes
officials of the Norwegian Industry and Energy Ministry
and President of the Statoil Company Harold Norving.

The Petroleum Information Agency learned that the
Statoil president and First Vice President of the Azerba-
ijani State Oil Company Sayad Ibragimov had signed an
agreement on cooperation in the sphere of extracting oil
and gas during the visit of the Norwegian delegation to
Baku. The agreement is expected to precede a plan for
long-time cooperation. The agreement provides for
exchanging experience in the sphere of environmental
protection and cooperation for preparing personnel.

Statoil takes part in the international consortium for
developing the Azerbaijani oil deposits in the Caspian
Sea and in the British Petroleum/Statoil alliance.

Experts said Azerbaijan had been recently attentively
examining the Norwegian legislation in the sphere of
extracting and processing oil and gas. (In 1963 Norway
declared sovereignty over its continental shelf).

The Azerbaijani parliament is now examining the Nor-
wegian oil legislation some principles of which could be
used for making an Azerbaijani law on oil. Azerbaijan
planned to adopt the law on oil by the end of this year.
However, experts said that it would take several months
more for working out the document.

Aliyev, Ter-Petrosyan To Meet in Moscow Next
Week

LD2411175294 Moscow INTERFAX in English 1538
GMT 24 Nov 94

[From the “Presidential Bulletin” feature; compiled by
Nikolay Zherebtsov, Andrey Petrovskiy, and edited by
Vladimir Shishlin]

[FBIS Transcribed Text] The meeting between Azeri
President Geydar Aliyev and his Armenian counterpart
Levon Ter-Petrosyan on the Karabakh settlement, with
the mediation of Russian President Yeltsin, is expected
to take place in Moscow in the middle of next week, a
senior source in Baku told Interfax. He said that the
parties concerned will meet before the CSTE summit to
be eld in Budapest on December 5-6.

It is so far unknown, said the source, whether the two
presidents will sign documents, though, he said, they are

This report may contain copyrighted material. Copying and dissemination
is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

68 CAUCASUS

being drafted. The main problem to be discussed in
Moscow is the problem of the towns of Shusha and
Lachin. Baku insists on their liberation from Armenian
units.

Armenia claims that it cannot agree to this since Shusha
is situated on the territory of Nagorno-Karabakh and the
only road connecting Nagorno-Karabakh with Armenia
passes across Lachin.

The Russian president’s special envoy for Nagorno-
Karabakh, Vladimir Kazimirov, voiced hope in an inter-
view with Interfax that the forthcoming meeting of the
three presidents will help “progress” in the work over the
Russ:a-proposed draft comprehensive political agree-
ment on the Karabakh settlement.

Russia is in favor of sending peace-keepers in Nagorno-
Karabakh only if they help concluding agreements on
cessation of the armed conflict when it is agreed upon by
the conflicting sides, said Kazimirov.

However, first it is necessary to work out international
and legal, and organizing and technical aspects of the
possible operation. According to the Russian diplomat,
those problems should not be solved in haste. This is the
wrong region for experimenting, he said.

The peace-keeping operation cannot be a goal in itself,
said Kazimirov. It should help concluding a political
agreement between the conflicting sides. He said that the
peace-keeping operation may, for its part, create more
favorable conditions for new agreements and a compre-
hensive settlement.

Such Russia’s position, said Kazimirov, was outlined in
Budapest November 18 at the meeting of the CSCE
Senior Officials Committee, which is studying the pos-
sibility of a peace-keeping operation. “A full-scale many-
component operation is a principally new form of peace-
keeping for the Conference on Security and Cooperation
in Europe,” he said.

Kazimirov spoke against attempts to oppose Russia and
the CSCE in the Karabakh settlement. We are for coor-
dination and harmonization of the efforts, he said. The
diplomat expressed regret that “‘some countries are
trying to press Russia, the CIS in the settlement process,
though major progress, beginning from the six-
month-long ceasefire, was achieved thanks to their medi-
atory efforts”. Kazimirov did not rule out that later
mediatory efforts of Russia, the Commonwealth of Inde-
pendent States and the CSCE will be “more integrated.”
IF Note: The aide of the Nagorno-Karabakh Foreign
Minister Manvell Sarkisiyan told Interfax that Ter-
Petrosyan is likely to insist on participation in the
forthcoming talks of chairman of the Karabakh Defense
Committee Robert Kocharyan who did not yet receive
official invitation. Meanwhile, observers in Stepanakert
believe that the talks in Moscow between the leaders of
Armenia, Azerbaijan and Russia are “merely the attach-
ment of importance to the Russian peace-keeping initia-
tive against the background of the CSCE intensified
efforts.”

Mobile Police Will Shoot to Kill in New
Crackdown

MK2411123094 Moscow NEZAVISIMAYA GAZETA in
Russian 24 Nov 94 p 3

[NEGA report in the “Georgia” column]

[FBIS Translated Text] The Georgia Ministry of Internal
Affairs is creating mobile armed police groups. This was
announced by republic Internal Affairs Minister Shota
Kviraya. This decision was made following increased
attacks on policemen. The groups will operate around
the clock. According to a release from the Georgia
Ministry of Internal Affairs Press Center, persons
offering armed resistance will be shot on the spot. The
press center release also said that over the last one and a
half years more than 200 police were killed in the line of
duty. The police have neutralized more than 1,000
armed gangs and detained over 3,000 criminals.

Estonia

Prime Minister Returns From Crime Conference

WS2511103194 Tallinn ETA in English 0820 GMT
25 Nov 94

[From 25 Nov EESTI SONUMID, PAEVALEHT p.1, 3;
“News Bulletin” No. 230/94]

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Prime Minister Andres Tarand
arrived back from a three day conference on fighting
organized crime, held in Naples by United Nations.

The conference had approved a political declaration and
planned activities for fighting organized crime, espe-
cially exchanging information and developing regional
cooperation. Prime Minister admitted that Estonia is
most interested in the cooperation of East-West Europe
as there are few crime lines that do not touch Estonia,
traffic of stolen cars being among the biggest problems,
but there have also been some attempts to smuggle
radioactive materials. These problems were the reasons
for Tarand’s meetings at the conference—with Finnish
Interior Minister Mauri Pekkarinen on cooperation and
training the specialists and with Russian Interior Min-
ister Viktor Yerin on handing over criminal offenders
and agreements, following the one already signed
between Estonia and Russia on providing legal advice.

The meeting with the Italian interior minister was “most
fertile of them” in Tarand’s opinion, “the important
message for Estonia being the clear support of Italy to the
progress by Estonia and other Baltic countries towards
the European Union.”

ee Ministry Statement on Yeltsin’s Border
isit

LD2311153694 Tallinn Radio Tallinn Network in
Estonian 1500 GMT 23 Nov 94

{[FBIS Translated Text] Russian President Boris
Yeltsin's visit today to a new Russian border post is an
example of Russia’s policy of unilateral steps on the
border issue, the Estonian Foreign Ministry concludes.
The Foreign Ministry has just issued a statement which
says that President Yeltsin’s visit is evidently linked to
Russian domestic politics. The statement says that the
Russian president's visit to the newly completed border
post along the Estonian-Russian control line again draws
attention to the need to resolve the Estonian-Russian
border issue under international law.

The Foreign Ministry states that Estonia has received no
reply to its request for talks which were called for by both
the prime minister and by deputies. It is also noted that
Estonia has not received an acequate reply to its initia-
tive to resolve the issue with the mediation of the
Conference on Security and Cooperation in Europe, or
any other international organization or country. The
Foreign Ministry promises to study all statements made
today by the Russian Federation’s president in detail and
calls for an end to the Russian policy of unilateral
actions.

Kozyrev: Border ‘Will Remain Unchanged’

WS2311143694 Tallinn BNS in English 1300 GMT
23 Nov 94

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Pskov, Russia, Nov 23, BNS—
Russian Foreign Minister Andrey Kozyrev who accom-
panied President Boris Yeltsin during his inspection tour
on the border with Estonia today criticized harshly
Estonia’s bid for establishing the line defined in the
Tartu Peace Treaty of 1920 as the border between the
two states.

“The present Russian-Estonian border will remain
unchanged and neighboring states must stop talking
about territorial claims,” the Russian foreign minister
told BNS. Kozyrev maintained that the Russian-
Estonian border was firmly fixed.

“The sooner our neighbors realize that their territorial
claims are baseless, the sooner it will be possible to mark
down the border properly,” he said.

In Kozyrev’s words, Russia is seeking, not tensions
caused by the border issue, but goodneighborly relations
that would “help protect Estonia, Latvia, and Russia
against smugglers, terrorists, and drug traffickers.” The
Russian foreign minister said the president’s visit to the
frontier was intended to promote “strengthening of the
Estonian-Russian border.”

[FBIS Translated Excerpt] At 1227, a Russian TU-134
aircraft landed at Tallinn airport, having been hijacked

according to preliminary information, three terrorists.
According to what is known, the aircraft crew was
threatened with a bomb-like object and told to fly either
to Amsterdam or Denmark. [passage omitted]

The Russian authorities were informed of the hijacking
at 1115 Estonian time, and half an hour previously the
hijackers had presented their demands to the pilots.
According to what the pilots say, there is a smell of
acetone and petrol in the passenger cabin.

The Estonian police, ambulances, the special K com-
mando unit, and rescue service units have arrived at
Tallinn airport. Also, riflemen of the rapid reaction
regiment for domestic purposes have been brought to the
airport. The airport remains closed for technical reasons
until 1300. This has been reported by the BALTIC

GMT 24 Nov 94

[By ITAR-TASS correspondent Aleksandr Nechayev]}

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Moscow November 24 TASS—
The TU-134 airliner with 70 people aboard was suppos-
edly hijacked by three men, the Estonian Transport
Department told ITAR-TASS on Thursday [24
November]. It is not known so far whether they are
armed. However, according to the department
spokesman, one of them has “an attache case with a
queer content”.

The hijackers put forth three demands: refuelling,
replacement of the flight navigator and flight for den-
mark. If their demands are met, they promised to release
all passengers, the department spokesman noted, adding
that talks are now being conducted with the hijackers “at
the top level”. The plane remains at the Tallinn airport.

Refuelling has not been made as yet. According to the
Tallinn airport security service, the pilots asked to bring
a bus to the plane to evacuate the passengers.

However, the transport department spokesman said that
no passengers were released for the time being. He added

“The Estonian special services resolved the hijacking of
the passenger aircraft in a professional manner. This fact
reinforces the important point that Estonia is not a
terrorist’s transit place,” Luik said according to Foreign
Ministry's press service.

“I hope that this message is understandable to all those
who would come to Estonia with bad intentions,” Luik
stressed.

The Estonian Government maintains the position that it
does not give in to or support terrorist demands, Foreign
Ministry stated.

is prohibited without permission of the copyright owners.

74 BALTIC STATES

Russian Envoy Grateful For Hijack Handling

LD2411174194 Tallinn Radio Tallinn Network in
Estonian 1700 GMT 24 Nov 94

[FBIS Translated Text] Russia’s Ambassador to Estonia
Aleksandr Trofimov thanked Foreign Minister Juri Luik
by telephone once the terrorist who had hijacked a
Russian plane had surrendered at Tallinn Airport. Luik
told the Baltic News Service that Trofimov thanked
Estonia for its precise and swift action in arresting the
terrorist and freeing the hostages.

The Estonian Foreign Ministry assumes that Russia will
be requesting the extradition of the terrorist and his
alleged accomplices.

Latvia

Premier Assesses Visit to Sweden

LD2311163194 Riga Radio Riga Network in Latvian
1401 GMT 23 Nov 94

[FBIS Translated Text] Prime Minister Maris Gailis
returned to Riga today from his first foreign visit in
Sweden. There Maris Gailis met with Prime Minister
Ingvar Carlsson, former prime minister Carl Bildt, and
people from the business world, and discussed security
issues in both Sweden and Latvia with officials from
security institutions.

Maris Gailis told reporters at the airport about the topics
at the most important talks:

[Begin Gailis recording] For an hour and a half we
discussed various things with Carlsson and of course the
main thing was security and the Baltic states’, Latvia’s,
and also Sweden’s integration into Europe. We know
that the referendum ended successfully, successfully for
those who believe that Sweden must be a member of the
European Union. Sweden will be a member of the
European Union from | January. We (?also) discussed
the possibility of Sweden helping Latvia in the integra-
tion process and there are possibilities of all kinds and
promises.

Of course they are very interested in our neighborly
relations with Russia and that was also a subject for
talks.

One of the government priorities is internal security and
it undoubtedly concerns the close neighbor state,
Sweden, because we know about the illegal immigration,
also about organized crime in Sweden. We talked about
cooperation between the police and also their technical
and financia! assistance to our police, our system of
prisons, assistance in creating the immigration police.

And in the interests of stability the Swedes are undoubt-
edly very interested, together with other world countries
and international organizations, in assisting us with the
language teaching program for our noncitizens, that is, in
the framework of naturalization.

FBIS-SOV-94-227
25 November 1994

We noted with satisfaction the fact of the recent opening
of Riga School of Economics and the Swedes will be
ready to find ways to help create another school—the law
school. [end recording}

A new idea of what should be done with the Skrunda
radar base has appeared in Latvia. At today’s news
conference the prime minister also answered a reporter's
questions about Maris Gailis’s ideas about the fate of the
new building at the Skrunda radar station: Should this
building be demolished or used differently?

[Begin Gailis recording] | think that the Skrunda radar
base must be demolished. That is related to international
agreements. It is also related to the assistance worth
many millions of dollars which comes from various
countries—from Sweden as well, by the way. The
transfer has not yet been effected due to all kinds of
technical issues, and the legal issue had not been brought
to order. Now that has been done, accounts have been
opened, the head of the fund has been approved, and
they promised to transfer the money.

But we all know that it is in the interests of security to
demolish the new buildings at the Skrunda radar facility,
because if they stay there, then if there is some kind of
political change the temptation may occur to finish the
facility which has been started. In the end, it is a political
decision. Maybe someone does not like it, but what can
be done? It is never possible to achieve full consensus on
these issues. [end recording]

That is what Prime Minister Maris Gailis said today on
his return from Sweden.

Baltic Foreign Ministers Statement on EU,
Borders

LD2311194094 Riga Radio Riga Network in Latvian
1800 GMT 23 Nov 94

[FBIS Translated Text] The foreign ministers of Latvia,
Lithuania, and Estonia welcome the decision of the
Council on General Affairs [as heard] of the European
Union [EU] to adopt a mandate project for talks with the
Baltic countries. In their joint statement, signed today by
the Baltic foreign ministers after their talks in Riga, they
express the conviction that this mandate will be con-
firmed by the session of the Council, to be held on 28th
and 29th November in Luxembourg, and this will be the
opportunity for Estonia, Latvia, and Lithuania to start
even this year on the first round of talks with the
European Commission.

The three Baltic foreign ministers, in the names of their
governments, also signed an agreement on cooperation
in issues of guarding state borders. The agreement pro-
vides for the creation of a data bank on issues concerning
the guarding the countries’ borders. Each of the three
Baltic countries intends to observe the interests of the
other countries in conducting border checks of transport
of people, cargoes, and passengers. A common mecha-
nism for border checks will be worked out, to strengthen
