Spokesman Rebuts Yeltsin Statement on ‘Forced
Settlers’

WS2511095694 Tallinn BNS in English 1600 GMT
24 Nov 94

[FBIS Transcribed Text] Riga, Nov 24, BNS—AIl per-
sons who leave Latvia for Russia do that voluntarily and
not as a result of pressure, Latvian Foreign Ministry
spokesman Roberts Mucins voiced his ministry's stance
on the issue Thursday [24 November].

Mucins made the comment in view of statements by
Russian President Boris Yeltsin during his trip to the
Pskov region Wednesday.

Yeltsin met with “forced settlers” from Latvia at the
Dubrov village near Pskov and told them that Russia
intended to demand Latvian help in building homes for
these people, according to the RIA news agency.

Latvia has not observed the existence of “forced set-
tlers,”’ or refugees, Mucins said.

Similar statements by the Russian president could be
construed as “use of foreign political factors in the
settlement of interior problems,” said Mucins.

Commenting on the tough Russian commitment not to
give away “a single centimeter” of its land, Mucins said
Latvia had no territorial claims to Russia.

Regarding the Abrene district, part of which was over-
taken by Russia and renamed Pitalovo in 1944, Latvia
wants the rights of former owners restored, easy frontier
crossing rules for frontier zone residents introduced and
other routine problems settled, Mucins said.

Accords Ratified on Russian Army Withdrawal

LD2411113794 Riga Radio Riga Network in Latvian
1100 GMT 24 Nov 94

[FBIS Translated Text] The Saeima [parliament] today
ratified the Russian-Latvian agreements on army with-
drawal and an interstate agreement on the protection of
rights of migrants. After the exchange of ratification
papers with Russia, the agreements will be sent to the
Conference on Security and Cooperation in Europe for
experts of that organization to be able to begin the
monitoring of the dismantling and work of the Skrunda
radar system. After the ratification of the Russian-
Latvian agreements, Latvia now has the legal basis to
begin expelling officers who remain here illegally after

BALTIC STATES 75

January 1992 [Russian officers dismissed from service
on Latvian territory after 28 January 1992 and still
remaining in Latvia}.

As is known, the two agreements have also been ratified
by the Russian parliament.

Saeima Passes Law on State Defense

LD2411140594 Riga Radio Riga Network in Latvian
1100 GMT 24 Nov 94

[FBIS Translated Text] The Saeima passed a law today
on state defense. The law brings to order the work of
elements of the Latvian armed forces, subordinating the
Civil Defense and the defense forces to the commander
of the national armed forces who is, in turn, subordinate
to the defense minister.

The Saeima also amended the laws on the defense forces
and the Civil Defense, corresponding to the law passed.

Defense Minister Janis Trapans stressed that the new
law is one of the cornerstones of the future security of the
Baltics and their further integration into the European
security structures. Within the next three-four months
the Defense Ministry will draft a concept of state security
which will be an introduction to the drafting of a military
doctrine.

Lithuania

Norway Supports Lithuania’s Admission to EU

LD2411115494 Vilnius Radio Vilnius International
Service in Lithuanian 2200 GMT 23 Nov 94

[FBIS Translated Text] Norway supports Lithuania’s
aspirations to join the European Union [EU], said Nor-
way’s Deputy Foreign Minister Svere Bjoerke on arrival
in Vilnius Wednesday [23 November]. The guest also
noted that in solving the Kaliningrad issues with Russia,
Norway urges Lithuania to seek a compromise.

According to Svere Bjoerke, Norway will never support
efforts to isolate Russia from the European processes.
She added that the Europe’s stability depends on how the
European countries cooperate with Russia.

According to Justas Paleckis, presidential adviser on
foreign policy, the view of the Norwegian deputy foreign
minister coincides with the view of Danish Foreign
Minister Nicis Petersen on the Kaliningrad and transit
problems. Niels Petersen stated during his visit to Vil-
nius that Lithuania and Russia should sign an agreement
on military transit to Kaliningrad through the territory
of Lithuania.
